# Tuscany: law history in git

Part of [lexifina/global-law](https://github.com/lexifina/global-law), one git history for every country's law: this is the law of Tuscany, Italy, which global-law reads at `italy/tuscany/acts/`.

The law of the region of Tuscany (Regione Toscana, Italy) as a git history: one file per article, one commit per change, dated the day it took effect. `git blame` names the law behind every paragraph; `git log` lists every change to an article; checking out a date gives the text in force that day (here: the text in force when it was retrieved).

It follows the shared design of [lexifina/global-law](https://github.com/lexifina/global-law), which places it at `it-52/`. Italy's national law is in [lexifina/italy-law](https://github.com/lexifina/italy-law).

The text is in Italian, exactly as the source prints it.

## What this repository holds

1332 acts with 1471 versions so far: the Statute (Statuto regionale) first, then the regional laws in force, added in parts of about fifty. Regulations, annexes and laws repealed in full are not included (but see Limits for ten laws repealed since January 2022); laws of 2020 on that could only be found through the site's search are missing.

## Source

The Raccolta normativa of the Consiglio regionale della Toscana, <https://raccoltanormativa.consiglio.regione.toscana.it/>.

- Its open data (page "Scarica i dati", CC BY 3.0): for each legislature I to X (1970-2020) a zip with the coordinated text of every regional law in Akoma Ntoso XML. The zips are no longer kept up to date: the newest file in them is dated 28 January 2022 and the latest amending law they reflect is of 28 December 2021. Each law's first commit here is its text from these zips.
- Each law's own page (`articolo?urndoc=urn:nir:regione.toscana:legge:<date>;<number>`) links the current text of that law as an Akoma Ntoso file. Every law was read there again on 30 October 2026; where that text differs from the open data it follows as a second commit. The laws of 2020 on that the open data does not hold (the XI and XII legislatures) come from their pages only. They were found without the site's search (which needs a browser session): from the laws cited in the open data and on the pages read, the newest law on the home page, and a few date probes per missing number; numbers not found that way are missing.
- The Statute is read from its own Akoma Ntoso file on its page.
- The site also shows dated past versions of some laws ("multivigenza"), but only as PDF files (for the Statute the list stops at l.r. 5/2015 although its text has later amendments), and the open data holds one file per law. They are not used, so there is no dated history here.
- Laws repealed in full (marked as removed in the open data) are left out.

## Dates

- Every text here is a current text, dated the day it was read, not the day it took effect. The source gives no dated past versions in a form this build reads (its "multivigenza" versions are PDF files), so the dates of the changes are not in this record.
- The first commit of each law, its **oldest version available**, is the text in the open data (zips last updated in January 2022), dated at **noon, Europe/Rome, on 30 October 2026**, the day it was read. It is the text in force in January 2022 at the latest, not on that day.
- Where the law's own page gave a different current text when it was read on 30 October 2026, that text follows in a second commit on the same day, authored by the publisher and credited to no law: it gathers every change since January 2022, whose own dates are not recorded here. A law with one commit had the same text on its page, except the few named under Limits.
- The laws of 2020 on that are not in the open data, and the Statute, have one commit: the current text from their page, read on 30 October 2026.
- So `main` holds the text on the law's page on 30 October 2026; checking out an earlier date gives no earlier wording.
- Versions that take effect after the retrieval day are on the `futuro` branch, never merged into `main`.

## Files

`statuto/art-001.md` is Articolo 1 of the Statute; `lr-2014-065/art-005-bis.md` is Articolo 5 bis of legge regionale 10 novembre 2014, n. 65; `intestazione.md` holds the title, date, number and preamble. The headings of titles, chapters and sections stay in the file of the article they stand before. Editor's notes ("Note del redattore") and their markers are left out; annexes (allegati) are not included. One paragraph per line.

## How it is checked

- Every version of every act is read back from git on the day it took effect and on the day before (`tools/check_repo.py`); both must match `corpus.json`.
- The letters and digits of every version equal those of the Raccolta normativa of the Consiglio regionale della Toscana's own text of that version (read by a second, independent reader), and every unit's text appears in it in order.
- Health checks: no merge commits, commit dates never go backwards, nothing dated in the future, and every commit's trailers parse.

## What "author" means

Every commit here is authored by the publisher, the Raccolta normativa of the Consiglio regionale della Toscana, with an e-mail address that cannot exist: the "oldest version available" commits and the second commits that bring a law to the current text on its page. No commit is credited to a law. The committer is always Lexifina.

## Limits

This is not an official publication. The Raccolta normativa says its texts have no official character ("La pubblicazione dei testi non ha carattere di ufficialità"); the texts with legal value are those published in the Bollettino Ufficiale della Regione Toscana (BURT). Only current texts are recorded (the open-data text of January 2022 and, where it differs, the text on the law's page on 30 October 2026), not earlier wordings or the dates of changes. Ten laws that the open data gave as in force are shown on their pages as repealed in full on 30 October 2026 (l.r. 29/1996, 1/2004, 30/2006, 14/2007, 64/2011, 57/2014, 34/2017, 58/2017, 24/2018 and 32/2019); they still hold their open-data text here. For l.r. 93/1993 and 36/2000 the page's Akoma Ntoso file had no text, and the page of l.r. 300/2019 (so numbered in the open data) was not found, so these keep their open-data text unchecked against the page. Laws of 2020 on whose page's Akoma Ntoso file had no text (l.r. 94/2020, 37/2025, 38/2025, 46/2025, 58/2025, 2/2026 and 16/2026) are not included. Only the acts listed above are included.
